   631	impl NamedTargetWire {
   632	    /// Project a runtime `NamedTarget` into the wire shape for serialization.
   633	    pub fn from_target(t: &NamedTarget) -> Self {
   634	        match t {
   635	            NamedTarget::Cell(addr) => NamedTargetWire::Cell {
   636	                sheet: addr.sheet,
   637	                row: addr.row,
   638	                col: addr.col,
   639	            },
   640	            NamedTarget::Range(r) => NamedTargetWire::Range {
   641	                sheet: r.sheet,
   642	                start_row: r.start_row,
   643	                start_col: r.start_col,
   644	                end_row: r.end_row,
   645	                end_col: r.end_col,
   646	            },
   647	            NamedTarget::Constant(v) => NamedTargetWire::Constant {
   648	                // NamedTarget::Constant(Value::Blank) is unusual but valid; map
   649	                // to Number(0.0) sentinel? No — preserve the variant by emitting
   650	                // Error("BLANK_SENTINEL")? Also wrong. The cleanest answer: refuse
   651	                // to serialize Blank constants — the user shouldn't have one,
   652	                // and we'd round-trip-lose it anyway.
   653	                //
   654	                // In practice, `from_value(&Value::Blank)` returns None. So to
   655	                // serialize a Blank constant we'd need a dedicated wire variant.
   656	                // For Phase 2A.8 we deny it at save time via the conversion
   657	                // helper below; this match arm assumes from_value returns Some.
   658	                value: CellWireValue::from_value(v).unwrap_or(CellWireValue::Pending),
   659	            },
   660	            NamedTarget::Formula(src) => NamedTargetWire::Formula {
   661	                source: src.as_ref().to_owned(),
   662	            },
   663	        }
   664	    }
   665	
   666	    /// Decode the wire shape back into a `NamedTarget`. Carries the name only
   667	    /// for error reporting; the caller embeds the result in a `NameTable`.
   668	    pub fn to_target(&self, name_for_error: &str) -> Result<NamedTarget, QbookError> {
   669	        match self {
   670	            NamedTargetWire::Cell { sheet, row, col } => {
   671	                if *row > MAX_ROW {
   672	                    return Err(QbookError::MalformedName {
   673	                        name: name_for_error.to_owned(),
   674	                        reason: format!("Cell.row {row} exceeds MAX_ROW {MAX_ROW}"),
   675	                    });
   676	                }
   677	                if *col > MAX_COLUMN {
   678	                    return Err(QbookError::MalformedName {
   679	                        name: name_for_error.to_owned(),
   680	                        reason: format!("Cell.col {col} exceeds MAX_COLUMN {MAX_COLUMN}"),
   681	                    });
   682	                }
   683	                Ok(NamedTarget::Cell(Address::new(*sheet, *row, *col)))
   684	            }
   685	            NamedTargetWire::Range {
   686	                sheet,
   687	                start_row,
   688	                start_col,
   689	                end_row,
   690	                end_col,
   691	            } => {
   692	                for (label, v, max) in [
   693	                    ("start_row", *start_row, MAX_ROW),
   694	                    ("start_col", *start_col, MAX_COLUMN),
   695	                    ("end_row", *end_row, MAX_ROW),
   696	                    ("end_col", *end_col, MAX_COLUMN),
   697	                ] {
   698	                    if v > max {
   699	                        return Err(QbookError::MalformedName {
   700	                            name: name_for_error.to_owned(),
   701	                            reason: format!("Range.{label} {v} exceeds max {max}"),
   702	                        });
   703	                    }
   704	                }
   705	                Ok(NamedTarget::Range(Range::new(
   706	                    *sheet, *start_row, *start_col, *end_row, *end_col,
   707	                )))
   708	            }
   709	            NamedTargetWire::Constant { value } => {
   710	                let v = value.to_value().map_err(|e| match e {
   711	                    QbookError::MalformedCell { detail, .. } => QbookError::MalformedName {
   712	                        name: name_for_error.to_owned(),
   713	                        reason: format!("Constant decode: {detail}"),
   714	                    },
   715	                    other => other,
   716	                })?;
   717	                Ok(NamedTarget::Constant(v))
   718	            }
   719	            NamedTargetWire::Formula { source } => {
   720	                Ok(NamedTarget::Formula(std::sync::Arc::from(source.as_str())))
   721	            }
   722	        }
   723	    }
   724	}

   726	/// Map `ErrorValue` to its canonical Excel-style text form (`#REF!`, `#VALUE!`, etc.).
   727	/// Used by both wire-format serialization and the user-visible representation per spec.
   728	pub fn error_to_canonical_text(e: ErrorValue) -> String {
   729	    match e {
   730	        ErrorValue::Ref => "#REF!".to_string(),
   731	        ErrorValue::Value => "#VALUE!".to_string(),
   732	        ErrorValue::NA => "#N/A".to_string(),
   733	        ErrorValue::DivZero => "#DIV/0!".to_string(),
   734	        ErrorValue::Null => "#NULL!".to_string(),
   735	        ErrorValue::Num => "#NUM!".to_string(),
   736	        ErrorValue::Name => "#NAME?".to_string(),
   737	        ErrorValue::Spill => "#SPILL!".to_string(),
   738	        ErrorValue::Calc => "#CALC!".to_string(),
   739	        ErrorValue::Disconnected => "#DISCONNECTED!".to_string(),
   740	        ErrorValue::Binding => "#BINDING!".to_string(),
   741	        ErrorValue::Timeout => "#TIMEOUT!".to_string(),
   742	        ErrorValue::Permission => "#PERMISSION!".to_string(),
   743	        ErrorValue::AINotAvailable => "#AI_NOT_AVAILABLE_V1".to_string(),
   744	        ErrorValue::Circ => "#CIRC!".to_string(),
   745	    }
   746	}
   747	
   748	fn parse_canonical_error_text(s: &str) -> Option<ErrorValue> {
   749	    Some(match s {
   750	        "#REF!" => ErrorValue::Ref,
   751	        "#VALUE!" => ErrorValue::Value,
   752	        "#N/A" => ErrorValue::NA,
   753	        "#DIV/0!" => ErrorValue::DivZero,
   754	        "#NULL!" => ErrorValue::Null,
   755	        "#NUM!" => ErrorValue::Num,
   756	        "#NAME?" => ErrorValue::Name,
   757	        "#SPILL!" => ErrorValue::Spill,
   758	        "#CALC!" => ErrorValue::Calc,
   759	        "#DISCONNECTED!" => ErrorValue::Disconnected,
   760	        "#BINDING!" => ErrorValue::Binding,
   761	        "#TIMEOUT!" => ErrorValue::Timeout,
   762	        "#PERMISSION!" => ErrorValue::Permission,
   763	        "#AI_NOT_AVAILABLE_V1" => ErrorValue::AINotAvailable,
   764	        "#CIRC!" => ErrorValue::Circ,
   765	        _ => return None,
   766	    })
   767	}

   769	/// Save a `Workbook` to `path` (a `.qbook/` directory). **Crash-safe atomic save**
   770	/// per the Phase 2A.8 megaudit H2 closure:
   771	///
   772	/// 1. Write the new workbook to `<path>.tmp-save-<random>`.
   773	/// 2. If `path` already exists, rename it to `<path>.bak-<random>`.
   774	/// 3. Rename the temp directory into `path`.
   775	/// 4. Best-effort remove the backup; on failure, log a warning to stderr but
   776	///    DO NOT silently swallow (megaudit M8). The next `load_workbook` will
   777	///    detect the orphan via `recover_from_crashed_save` and clean it up.
   778	///
   779	/// **Invariant**: between any two adjacent steps, at least one of `path` or
   780	/// `<path>.bak-<random>` contains a complete valid workbook. A crash at any
   781	/// instant leaves a recoverable on-disk state.
   782	///
   783	/// Cross-filesystem note: the temp and backup paths are *siblings* of the
   784	/// target, sharing its parent directory. POSIX `rename(2)` is atomic within a
   785	/// single filesystem; the sibling-only design preserves that property.
   786	pub fn save_workbook(wb: &Workbook, name: &str, path: &Path) -> Result<(), QbookError> {
   787	    save_workbook_extending(wb, name, path, |_| Ok(()))
   788	}
   789	
   790	/// Phase 2A.3.c (2026-05-12): closure-based extension primitive. Same atomic-save
   791	/// protocol as `save_workbook`, with an additional hook (`extend`) that fires
   792	/// AFTER `write_workbook_to_dir` populates the temp directory but BEFORE the
   793	/// atomic `rename(temp → target)`. Used by `ql_oplog::save_workbook_with_oplog`
   794	/// to write `oplog.bin` as a sidecar — it rides the same atomic rename and
   795	/// recovers via the same crash-rollback path.
   796	///
   797	/// The closure receives the temp directory path. It can `fs::write` arbitrary
   798	/// files into it. The atomic-save invariant still holds: at every step
   799	/// boundary, at least one of {target, `<target>.bak-<random>`} contains a
   800	/// complete valid workbook + any sidecars the closure wrote.
   801	///
   802	/// Closure failure cleans up the temp directory and propagates the error —
   803	/// same semantics as a `write_workbook_to_dir` failure.
   804	///
   805	/// Public `save_workbook` is a thin wrapper that calls this with a no-op
   806	/// closure; callers without sidecars should keep using `save_workbook`.
   807	pub fn save_workbook_extending<F>(
   808	    wb: &Workbook,
   809	    name: &str,
   810	    path: &Path,
   811	    extend: F,
   812	) -> Result<(), QbookError>
   813	where
   814	    F: FnOnce(&Path) -> Result<(), QbookError>,
   815	{
   816	    let paths = make_save_paths(path)?;
   817	
   818	    // Phase 2A.13 audit cycle-3 H5: refuse to save into a path that exists
   819	    // but is NOT a directory. `path.exists() && !path.is_dir()` means a
   820	    // regular file (or symlink, FIFO, etc.) is sitting where the workbook
   821	    // directory should be. The previous code would have happily renamed
   822	    // that file to `<base>.bak-<suffix>`, then installed our directory in
   823	    // its place — the user's original file becomes an orphan no one will
   824	    // ever find (recovery would try `remove_dir_all` on it and fail with
   825	    // `NotADirectory`). Loud refusal is the right answer.
   826	    if path.exists() && !path.is_dir() {
   827	        return Err(QbookError::InvalidPath {
   828	            path: path.to_path_buf(),
   829	            reason: "target exists but is not a directory",
   830	        });
   831	    }
   832	
   833	    // Step 1: write everything to the temp dir. If this fails, the original
   834	    // target (if any) is untouched and we clean up the partial temp.
   835	    if let Err(e) = write_workbook_to_dir(wb, name, &paths.temp) {
   836	        if let Err(cleanup_err) = fs::remove_dir_all(&paths.temp) {
   837	            // The original save error is what the caller wants; the cleanup
   838	            // failure goes to stderr per the M8 audit (don't silence).
   839	            eprintln!(
   840	                "warning: failed to clean partial temp directory {:?} after save error: {cleanup_err}",
   841	                paths.temp
   842	            );
   843	        }
   844	        return Err(e);
   845	    }
   846	
   847	    // Phase 2A.3.c: extension hook fires after the engine has finished
   848	    // populating the temp dir (envelope + sheets + marker), but before the
   849	    // atomic rename. Sidecars written here become part of the all-or-nothing
   850	    // visibility guarantee.
   851	    if let Err(e) = extend(&paths.temp) {
   852	        if let Err(cleanup_err) = fs::remove_dir_all(&paths.temp) {
   853	            eprintln!(
   854	                "warning: failed to clean partial temp directory {:?} after extend error: {cleanup_err}",
   855	                paths.temp
   856	            );
   857	        }
   858	        return Err(e);
   859	    }
   860	
   861	    // Step 2: if target exists, move it aside (NOT remove). After this, the
   862	    // old state is at `.bak-<random>` and the target is missing.
   863	    let had_existing = path.exists();
   864	    if had_existing {
   865	        fs::rename(path, &paths.backup)?;
   866	    }
   867	
   868	    // Step 3: install the new state at the target. If this fails after step 2,
   869	    // we have `.bak-<random>` only and no target — recovery on next load will
   870	    // roll back. To avoid leaving the temp dir orphaned in that case, attempt
   871	    // a rollback ourselves before returning.
   872	    if let Err(install_err) = fs::rename(&paths.temp, path) {
   873	        // Phase 2A.13 audit cycle-3 M5: when install fails AND restore also
   874	        // fails, surface a compound error (was: silently `eprintln!`ed the
   875	        // restore failure and returned only the install error).
   876	        let restore_err: Option<std::io::Error> = if had_existing {
   877	            fs::rename(&paths.backup, path).err()
   878	        } else {
   879	            None
   880	        };
   881	        // The temp dir didn't move into place; try to clean it up.
   882	        if let Err(cleanup_err) = fs::remove_dir_all(&paths.temp) {
   883	            eprintln!(
   884	                "warning: failed to clean temp directory {:?} after rename failure: {cleanup_err}",
   885	                paths.temp
   886	            );
   887	        }
   888	        if let Some(restore_err) = restore_err {
   889	            // Both failed → compound user-visible error.
   890	            return Err(QbookError::AtomicSaveRollbackFailed {
   891	                backup_path: paths.backup.clone(),
   892	                install: install_err.to_string(),
   893	                restore: restore_err.to_string(),
   894	            });
   895	        }
   896	        // Restore succeeded (or no prior target existed): the user's prior
   897	        // workbook is intact at `path`. Return the install error so the
   898	        // caller knows the save itself failed.
   899	        return Err(QbookError::Io(install_err));
   900	    }
   901	
   902	    // Step 4: best-effort backup cleanup. On failure, log loudly — but the
   903	    // save itself succeeded, so we return Ok. `recover_from_crashed_save` on
   904	    // a future load will eventually clean up the orphan.
   905	    if had_existing {
   906	        if let Err(e) = fs::remove_dir_all(&paths.backup) {
   907	            eprintln!(
   908	                "warning: save succeeded but backup cleanup failed at {:?}: {e}",
   909	                paths.backup
   910	            );
   911	        }
   912	    }
   913	    Ok(())
   914	}
   915	
   916	/// Phase 2A.8 audit M10: each save uses a fresh, collision-resistant suffix so
   917	/// concurrent saves from two threads in the same process never share temp /
   918	/// backup paths. The suffix mixes wall-clock nanos, PID, and the calling
   919	/// thread's id — sufficient uniqueness for the IDE save-path workload without
   920	/// pulling in a random-source crate.
   921	fn save_session_suffix() -> String {
   922	    let nanos = std::time::SystemTime::now()
   923	        .duration_since(std::time::UNIX_EPOCH)
   924	        .map(|d| d.as_nanos() as u64)
   925	        .unwrap_or(0);
   926	    let pid = std::process::id() as u64;
   927	    // ThreadId formats as "ThreadId(N)" — hash via length + a fixed mixer to
   928	    // get a u64 contribution. Stable within a process.
   929	    let tid_str = format!("{:?}", std::thread::current().id());
   930	    let tid_hash = tid_str.bytes().fold(0u64, |acc, b| {
   931	        acc.wrapping_mul(0x100000001B3).wrapping_add(b as u64)
   932	    });
   933	    let mixed = nanos
   934	        .wrapping_mul(0x9E3779B97F4A7C15)
   935	        .wrapping_add(pid.wrapping_mul(0xBF58476D1CE4E5B9))
   936	        ^ tid_hash;
   937	    format!("{mixed:016x}")
   938	}
   939	
   940	/// Resolved temp + backup paths for one save invocation. Sharing the suffix
   941	/// between temp and backup lets `recover_from_crashed_save` link them
   942	/// unambiguously if a crash occurs mid-protocol.
   943	struct SavePaths {
   944	    temp: PathBuf,
   945	    backup: PathBuf,
   946	}
   947	
   948	/// Derive temp + backup sibling paths for `target`. Phase 2A.8 audit M9
   949	/// closure: degenerate paths (no parent, no file_name) error loudly via
   950	/// `QbookError::InvalidPath` rather than the prior `unwrap_or` silent fallback.
   951	fn make_save_paths(target: &Path) -> Result<SavePaths, QbookError> {
   952	    let parent = target.parent().ok_or_else(|| QbookError::InvalidPath {
   953	        path: target.to_path_buf(),
   954	        reason: "no parent directory",
   955	    })?;
   956	    let basename = target
   957	        .file_name()
   958	        .map(|s| s.to_string_lossy().into_owned())
   959	        .ok_or_else(|| QbookError::InvalidPath {
   960	            path: target.to_path_buf(),
   961	            reason: "no file name",
   962	        })?;
   963	    // Refuse to save into a target whose basename is itself a temp/backup name
   964	    // — that would let a path like `foo.bak-abc/` collide with our own
   965	    // backup-rename protocol. Phase 2A.8 conservative gate; the IDE shouldn't
   966	    // pick names like this, but a hostile caller could.
   967	    if basename.contains(".tmp-save-") || basename.contains(".bak-") {
   968	        return Err(QbookError::InvalidPath {
   969	            path: target.to_path_buf(),
   970	            reason: "basename collides with atomic-save protocol naming",
   971	        });
   972	    }
   973	    let suffix = save_session_suffix();
   974	    Ok(SavePaths {
   975	        temp: parent.join(format!("{basename}.tmp-save-{suffix}")),
   976	        backup: parent.join(format!("{basename}.bak-{suffix}")),
   977	    })
   978	}

  1642	    // Phase 2A.8: hydrate the workbook's NameTable from the envelope's
  1643	    // `names` section. v1 envelopes lack the section (envelope.names is None);
  1644	    // those workbooks load with an empty NameTable, matching prior behavior.
  1645	    //
  1646	    // Phase 2A.9 audit M6: `Workbook::set_name` may now refuse reserved names
  1647	    // (currently `AI` per CORR-06). A v2 file that somehow contains a reserved
  1648	    // name (e.g., hand-edited TOML) surfaces as `QbookError::MalformedName`.

  1653	    // analog of the op-log replay's `InvalidSheet` — we can't carry the
  1654	    // structural error through `QbookError`'s vocabulary without a new

  1754	    // Phase 2A.13 audit cycle-3 H4: warn once per load if we rewrote any v1
  1755	    // `Error("#NULL!") + formula` cells to Pending. The rewrite is correct
  1756	    // for engine-produced files (where #NULL! was a stand-in for "not yet
  1757	    // evaluated") but COULD silently corrupt legitimate Excel #NULL! results
  1758	    // — the v1 wire format conflated both cases. The user should re-check
  1759	    // any formulas that legitimately produce #NULL! after this load.
  1760	    if legacy_pending_migrations > 0 {
  1761	        eprintln!(
  1762	            "warning: load_workbook {path:?} migrated {legacy_pending_migrations} v1 \
  1763	             `Error(#NULL!) + formula` cells to the v2 Pending semantic. \
  1764	             If any of those formulas were authored to produce #NULL! intentionally \
  1765	             (e.g. `=SUM(A1 B1)` with a space), their evaluated values will be lost \
  1766	             until recompute. Re-save the workbook in v2 format to remove this ambiguity."
  1767	        );
  1768	    }
  1769	
  1770	    Ok(wb)
  1771	}

  1801	    #[test]
  1802	    fn roundtrip_empty_workbook_one_sheet() {
  1803	        let dir = TempDir::new().unwrap();
  1804	        let path = dir.path().join("test.qbook");
  1805	        let wb = wb_with("Sheet1", &[]);
  1806	        save_workbook(&wb, "test", &path).unwrap();
  1807	
  1808	        let loaded = load_workbook(&path).unwrap();
  1809	        assert_eq!(loaded.sheet_count(), 1);

  1811	    }

   980	/// Phase 2A.8 audit H2 recovery protocol. Called at the top of
   981	/// `load_workbook`. Detects orphan `.bak-<random>` siblings of `target` and
   982	/// either rolls forward (target is intact, just clean up bak) or rolls back
   983	/// (target missing/invalid, rename bak → target).
   984	///
   985	/// If multiple `.bak-*` orphans exist (unlikely; would require multiple
   986	/// crashes across save sessions), we conservatively refuse to load and ask
   987	/// the caller to manually resolve. Returning an error here is safer than
   988	/// guessing which backup is "the right one."
   989	fn recover_from_crashed_save(target: &Path) -> Result<(), QbookError> {
   990	    let Some(parent) = target.parent() else {
   991	        return Ok(()); // No parent, nothing to scan.
   992	    };
   993	    let basename = match target.file_name() {
   994	        Some(s) => s.to_string_lossy().into_owned(),
   995	        None => return Ok(()),
   996	    };
   997	    let bak_prefix = format!("{basename}.bak-");
   998	
   999	    // Scan the parent dir for orphan .bak-* siblings.
  1000	    // Phase 2A.13 audit cycle-3 H1: ONLY consider directories that carry our
  1001	    // atomic-save marker file (`is_engine_owned_backup`). A user-created
  1002	    // sibling like `book.qbook.bak-2025-review` shares the filename prefix
  1003	    // but lacks the marker — recovery leaves it alone.
  1004	    let mut orphans: Vec<PathBuf> = Vec::new();
  1005	    let read_dir = match fs::read_dir(parent) {
  1006	        Ok(it) => it,
  1007	        Err(_) => return Ok(()), // Parent unreadable; load will fail downstream with a clearer error.
  1008	    };
  1009	    for entry in read_dir.flatten() {
  1010	        if let Some(name) = entry.file_name().to_str() {
  1011	            if name.starts_with(&bak_prefix) {
  1012	                let p = entry.path();
  1013	                if is_engine_owned_backup(&p) {

  1015	                }

  1017	                // `.bak-*` prefix. Not ours, don't touch.
  1018	            }
  1019	        }

  1023	        return Ok(());
  1024	    }

  1027	    // multiple orphans, they're ALL stale (a valid target can only result
  1028	    // from a single most-recent successful save; any older `.bak-*` is from
  1029	    // a prior save whose step-4 cleanup never completed). Clean them all up

  1031	    // repeated step-4 failures would otherwise create a permanent deadlock.
  1032	    if target_envelope_is_valid(target) {

   823	/// points: any structured ref surfaces as
   824	/// `BindError::UnknownTable(name)`.
   825	pub(crate) struct EmptyTableLookup;
   826	
   827	impl TableLookup for EmptyTableLookup {

   832	
   833	/// **W5-99 (Phase 4.7.F):** human-readable variant tag for the
   834	/// `ArrayCellNotLiteral` error message. Used only on the error
   835	/// path so the cost is irrelevant.
   836	/// **W5-115 (Phase 4.8.F):** resolve a parsed structured reference
   837	/// against the table registry. Produces `ExprPlan::StructuredRef`
   838	/// with a concrete `Range` (eval reads this) plus a flag indicating
   839	/// whether the row needs per-cell narrowing (`[@Col]` forms).
   840	///
   841	/// Resolution rules per design § 7.2:
   842	/// - `BareColumn(col)` → column's data range (rows excluding
   843	///   header/totals).
   844	/// - `Combination([...])` — normalize to a single (row selector,
   845	///   column selector) intersection. Multiple `Special` items combine
   846	///   as union over rows; multiple `Column` items must be contiguous
   847	///   (Excel canon — no gappy ranges). Currently supports the common
   848	///   forms: single Special, single Column, single ColumnRange,
   849	///   `[#Special], [Col]`, `[#Special], [Col1]:[Col2]`. Multi-Special
   850	///   combinations like `[#Headers], [#Data]` lower to a single union
   851	///   range when possible (e.g., `headers ∪ data` = `all - totals`).
   852	/// - `ThisRowColumn(col)` / `ThisRowColumnRange(c1, c2)` → column's
   853	///   full data range with `is_this_row: true`. Eval narrows.
   854	fn resolve_structured_ref(
   855	    table_name: &Arc<str>,
   856	    spec: &ql_formula_syntax::TableSpecSubtree,
   857	    tables: &dyn TableLookup,
   858	) -> Result<ExprPlan, BindError> {
   859	    use ql_formula_syntax::TableSpecSubtree;
   860	    let table = tables
   861	        .lookup_table(table_name.as_ref())
   862	        .ok_or_else(|| BindError::UnknownTable(Arc::clone(table_name)))?;
   863	    let canonical_name = Arc::clone(&table.name);
   864	    // Helper closures for the common error paths.
   865	    let unknown_column = |col: &Arc<str>| BindError::UnknownTableColumn {
   866	        table: Arc::clone(&canonical_name),
   867	        column: Arc::clone(col),
   868	    };
   869	
   870	    let (resolved, is_this_row) = match spec {
   871	        TableSpecSubtree::BareColumn(col) => {
   872	            let (idx, _) = table
   873	                .lookup_column(col)
   874	                .ok_or_else(|| unknown_column(col))?;
   875	            let range = table.column_data_range(idx).ok_or_else(|| {
   876	                BindError::StructuredRefDegenerateRange(Arc::clone(&canonical_name))
   877	            })?;
   878	            (range, false)
   879	        }
   880	        TableSpecSubtree::ThisRowColumn(col) => {
   881	            let (idx, _) = table
   882	                .lookup_column(col)
   883	                .ok_or_else(|| unknown_column(col))?;
   884	            // Eval narrows to one cell; binder hands back the full data
   885	            // column. If the column has no data rows, the eval narrow
   886	            // returns degenerate → cell-boundary maps to #VALUE!
   887	            // (handled at 4.8.G).
   888	            let range = table.column_data_range(idx).ok_or_else(|| {
   889	                BindError::StructuredRefDegenerateRange(Arc::clone(&canonical_name))
   890	            })?;
   891	            (range, true)
   892	        }
   893	        TableSpecSubtree::ThisRowColumnRange(c1, c2) => {
   894	            let (i1, _) = table.lookup_column(c1).ok_or_else(|| unknown_column(c1))?;
   895	            let (i2, _) = table.lookup_column(c2).ok_or_else(|| unknown_column(c2))?;
   896	            let lo = i1.min(i2);
   897	            let hi = i1.max(i2);
   898	            let r1 = table.column_data_range(lo).ok_or_else(|| {
   899	                BindError::StructuredRefDegenerateRange(Arc::clone(&canonical_name))
   900	            })?;
   901	            let r2 = table.column_data_range(hi).ok_or_else(|| {
   902	                BindError::StructuredRefDegenerateRange(Arc::clone(&canonical_name))
   903	            })?;
   904	            (
   905	                Range::new(r1.sheet, r1.start_row, r1.start_col, r2.end_row, r2.end_col),
   906	                true,
   907	            )
   908	        }
   909	        TableSpecSubtree::Combination(items) => {
   910	            resolve_sref_combination(&canonical_name, table, items)?
   911	        }
   912	    };
   913	
   914	    Ok(ExprPlan::StructuredRef {
   915	        table_name: canonical_name,
   916	        source: Arc::new(spec.clone()),
   917	        resolved,
   918	        is_this_row,
   919	    })
   920	}

   922	/// **W5-115 (Phase 4.8.F):** resolve a `Combination` spec to a single
   923	/// row-selector × column-selector range. Common forms:
   924	/// - `[#All]` → full footprint.
   925	/// - `[#Headers]` → header row across all columns.
   926	/// - `[#Totals]` → totals row.
   927	/// - `[#Data]` → data rows.
   928	/// - `[Col]` → single column data.
   929	/// - `[Col1]:[Col2]` → column range data.
   930	/// - `[#Special], [Col]` → intersection (e.g. `[#Headers], [Qty]` →
   931	///   header cell of Qty column).
   932	/// - `[#Special], [Col1]:[Col2]` → intersection over column range.
   933	fn resolve_sref_combination(
   934	    canonical: &Arc<str>,
   935	    table: &ql_storage::TableMetadata,
   936	    items: &[ql_formula_syntax::TableSpecItem],
   937	) -> Result<(Range, bool), BindError> {
   938	    use ql_formula_syntax::{SpecialItem, TableSpecItem};
   939	
   940	    // Walk items: gather special items (row selectors) and column items.
   941	    let mut row_selectors: Vec<SpecialItem> = Vec::new();
   942	    let mut col_items: Vec<&TableSpecItem> = Vec::new();
   943	    for it in items {
   944	        match it {
   945	            TableSpecItem::Special(s) => row_selectors.push(*s),
   946	            other => col_items.push(other),
   947	        }
   948	    }
   949	
   950	    // Compute the row range.
   951	    let row_range = compute_row_range(canonical, table, &row_selectors)?;
   952	
   953	    // Compute the column range.
   954	    let col_range = compute_col_range(canonical, table, &col_items)?;
   955	
   956	    // Intersect.
   957	    let resolved = Range::new(
   958	        table.sheet,
   959	        row_range.0,
   960	        col_range.0,
   961	        row_range.1,
   962	        col_range.1,
   963	    );
   964	
   965	    // `[[#This Row], [Col]]` → is_this_row. Detected by exactly one
   966	    // ThisRow item in selectors. Eval narrows.
   967	    let is_this_row = row_selectors.contains(&SpecialItem::ThisRow);
   968	
   969	    Ok((resolved, is_this_row))
   970	}
   971	
   972	/// Resolve the row span from a list of `Special` items. Empty list
   973	/// defaults to the data range (Excel canon for column-only specs).
   974	fn compute_row_range(
   975	    canonical: &Arc<str>,
   976	    table: &ql_storage::TableMetadata,
   977	    selectors: &[ql_formula_syntax::SpecialItem],
   978	) -> Result<(ql_types::RowId, ql_types::RowId), BindError> {
   979	    use ql_formula_syntax::SpecialItem;
   980	    if selectors.is_empty() {
   981	        let data = table
   982	            .data_range()
   983	            .ok_or_else(|| BindError::StructuredRefDegenerateRange(Arc::clone(canonical)))?;
   984	        return Ok((data.start_row, data.end_row));
   985	    }
   986	    // Union over rows. The 5 selectors:
   987	    let mut min_row: Option<ql_types::RowId> = None;
   988	    let mut max_row: Option<ql_types::RowId> = None;
   989	    let mut accumulate = |r: Range| {
   990	        min_row = Some(min_row.map_or(r.start_row, |m| m.min(r.start_row)));
   991	        max_row = Some(max_row.map_or(r.end_row, |m| m.max(r.end_row)));
   992	    };
   993	    for sel in selectors {
   994	        let r = match sel {
   995	            SpecialItem::Headers => table
   996	                .header_range()
   997	                .ok_or_else(|| BindError::TableHasNoHeader(Arc::clone(canonical)))?,
   998	            SpecialItem::Totals => table
   999	                .totals_range()
  1000	                .ok_or_else(|| BindError::TableHasNoTotals(Arc::clone(canonical)))?,
  1001	            SpecialItem::Data => table
  1002	                .data_range()
  1003	                .ok_or_else(|| BindError::StructuredRefDegenerateRange(Arc::clone(canonical)))?,
  1004	            SpecialItem::All => table.all_range(),
  1005	            SpecialItem::ThisRow => {
  1006	                // ThisRow as a row selector inside a Combination means
  1007	                // "the formula's current row". The binder defers actual
  1008	                // row resolution to eval time; here we hand back the
  1009	                // full data range and rely on the caller setting
  1010	                // is_this_row.
  1011	                table
  1012	                    .data_range()
  1013	                    .ok_or_else(|| BindError::StructuredRefDegenerateRange(Arc::clone(canonical)))?
  1014	            }
  1015	        };
  1016	        accumulate(r);
  1017	    }
  1018	    Ok((
  1019	        min_row.expect("at least one selector accumulated"),
  1020	        max_row.expect("at least one selector accumulated"),
  1021	    ))
  1022	}

   749	/// **W5-89 (Phase 4.6.A part 3):** attach a sheet name from `Sheet!Ref`
   750	/// syntax to the inner reference term. The inner term must be a
   751	/// reference shape (CellRef / RangeRef::*). Anything else is a parse
   752	/// error. If the inner reference already has a non-`Current` sheet,
   753	/// that's `DoubleSheetQualifier` (`Sheet1!Sheet2!X`).
   754	fn apply_sheet_to_term(expr: Expr, name: Arc<str>) -> Result<Expr, ParseError> {
   755	    // **W5-89:** `Sheet1!5:10` — the lexer emits `Number(5)` for bare
   756	    // digits. The whole-row range conversion happens in `build_range`
   757	    // when the `:` infix triggers. Up-front, we convert here so the
   758	    // sheet prefix attaches to the resulting `WholeRow` singleton (and
   759	    // the subsequent range merge keeps the prefix). Non-Number Exprs
   760	    // pass through unchanged.
   761	    let expr = if matches!(expr, Expr::Number(_)) {
   762	        number_to_bare_row(expr)?
   763	    } else {
   764	        expr
   765	    };
   766	    match expr {
   767	        Expr::CellRef(addr) => {
   768	            if !matches!(addr.sheet, SheetRef::Current) {
   769	                return Err(ParseError::DoubleSheetQualifier);
   770	            }
   771	            Ok(Expr::CellRef(CellAddr {
   772	                sheet: SheetRef::Name(name),
   773	                ..addr
   774	            }))
   775	        }
   776	        Expr::RangeRef(r) => {
   777	            let updated = match r {
   778	                RangeRef::Cells {
   779	                    sheet,
   780	                    start_col,
   781	                    start_row,
   782	                    end_col,
   783	                    end_row,
   784	                    abs_start_col,
   785	                    abs_start_row,
   786	                    abs_end_col,
   787	                    abs_end_row,
   788	                } => {
   789	                    if !matches!(sheet, SheetRef::Current) {
   790	                        return Err(ParseError::DoubleSheetQualifier);
   791	                    }
   792	                    RangeRef::Cells {
   793	                        sheet: SheetRef::Name(name),
   794	                        start_col,
   795	                        start_row,
   796	                        end_col,
   797	                        end_row,
   798	                        abs_start_col,
   799	                        abs_start_row,
   800	                        abs_end_col,
   801	                        abs_end_row,
   802	                    }
   803	                }
   804	                RangeRef::WholeColumn {
   805	                    sheet,
   806	                    start_col,
   807	                    end_col,
   808	                    abs_start,
   809	                    abs_end,
   810	                } => {
   811	                    if !matches!(sheet, SheetRef::Current) {
   812	                        return Err(ParseError::DoubleSheetQualifier);
   813	                    }
   814	                    RangeRef::WholeColumn {
   815	                        sheet: SheetRef::Name(name),
   816	                        start_col,
   817	                        end_col,
   818	                        abs_start,
   819	                        abs_end,
   820	                    }
   821	                }
   822	                RangeRef::WholeRow {
   823	                    sheet,
   824	                    start_row,
   825	                    end_row,
   826	                    abs_start,
   827	                    abs_end,
   828	                } => {
   829	                    if !matches!(sheet, SheetRef::Current) {
   830	                        return Err(ParseError::DoubleSheetQualifier);
   831	                    }
   832	                    RangeRef::WholeRow {
   833	                        sheet: SheetRef::Name(name),
   834	                        start_row,
   835	                        end_row,
   836	                        abs_start,
   837	                        abs_end,
   838	                    }
   839	                }
   840	            };
   841	            Ok(Expr::RangeRef(updated))
   842	        }
   843	        other => Err(ParseError::SheetQualifierFollowedByNonReference {
   844	            got: format!("{other:?}"),
   845	        }),
   846	    }
   847	}

   849	/// **W5-89 (Phase 4.6.A part 3):** merge two endpoint `SheetRef`s for
   850	/// range construction per design § 10.5:
   851	///
   852	/// - `(Current, Current)` → `Current` (same-sheet range).
   853	/// - `(Name(N), Current)` → `Name(N)` (prefix applies to whole range).
   854	/// - `(Current, Name(_))` → `MixedSheetRangeEndpoints` (Excel requires
   855	///   the prefix at the start, not on the trailing endpoint).
   856	/// - `(Name(A), Name(B))` with `A != B` (case-insensitive) →
   857	///   `MixedSheetRangeEndpoints`.
   858	/// - `(Name(A), Name(A))` → `Name(A)` (redundant explicit form,
   859	///   normalized to single-prefix).
   860	/// - `Id` variants are produced post-bind / by tests; treated symmetrically.
   861	fn merge_sheet_refs(lhs: &SheetRef, rhs: &SheetRef) -> Result<SheetRef, ParseError> {
   862	    match (lhs, rhs) {
   863	        (SheetRef::Current, SheetRef::Current) => Ok(SheetRef::Current),
   864	        (SheetRef::Name(n), SheetRef::Current) => Ok(SheetRef::Name(n.clone())),
   865	        (SheetRef::Id(s), SheetRef::Current) => Ok(SheetRef::Id(*s)),
   866	        (SheetRef::Current, SheetRef::Name(_) | SheetRef::Id(_)) => {
   867	            Err(ParseError::MixedSheetRangeEndpoints)
   868	        }
   869	        (SheetRef::Name(a), SheetRef::Name(b)) => {
   870	            if a.eq_ignore_ascii_case(b.as_ref()) {
   871	                Ok(SheetRef::Name(a.clone()))
   872	            } else {
   873	                Err(ParseError::MixedSheetRangeEndpoints)
   874	            }
   875	        }
   876	        (SheetRef::Id(a), SheetRef::Id(b)) if a == b => Ok(SheetRef::Id(*a)),
   877	        // Cross-variant mismatch (Name vs Id) or different Id values.
   878	        _ => Err(ParseError::MixedSheetRangeEndpoints),
   879	    }
   880	}
   881	
   882	/// **W5-89 (Phase 4.6.A part 3):** extract the `SheetRef` field from
   883	/// a reference-shaped Expr. Returns `Current` for non-reference shapes
   884	/// so the caller's pattern-match logic stays clean (those branches
   885	/// fail earlier with type-mismatch errors anyway).
   886	fn sheet_of_term(expr: &Expr) -> SheetRef {
   887	    match expr {
   888	        Expr::CellRef(addr) => addr.sheet.clone(),
   889	        Expr::RangeRef(RangeRef::Cells { sheet, .. })
   890	        | Expr::RangeRef(RangeRef::WholeColumn { sheet, .. })
   891	        | Expr::RangeRef(RangeRef::WholeRow { sheet, .. }) => sheet.clone(),
   892	        _ => SheetRef::Current,
   893	    }
   894	}
   895	
   896	/// Convert a literal `Number(N)` to a `RangeRef::WholeRow` singleton (1-indexed in source
   897	/// → 0-indexed internal). Used in range parsing for the `1:5` whole-row pattern. Other
   898	/// Expr variants pass through unchanged.
   899	fn number_to_bare_row(e: Expr) -> Result<Expr, ParseError> {
   900	    if let Expr::Number(n) = e {
   901	        // Excel row indices are 1-based positive integers in source. Validate:
   902	        if n.fract() != 0.0 || n < 1.0 || n > (u32::MAX as f64) {
   903	            return Err(ParseError::InvalidRange {
   904	                detail: "row index must be a positive integer",
   905	            });
   906	        }
   907	        let row = (n as u32) - 1;
   908	        Ok(Expr::RangeRef(RangeRef::WholeRow {
   909	            sheet: SheetRef::Current,
   910	            start_row: row,
   911	            end_row: row,
   912	            abs_start: false,
   913	            abs_end: false,
   914	        }))
   915	    } else {
   916	        Ok(e)
   917	    }
   918	}

   920	/// Infix-operator binding powers. Returns `None` for tokens that aren't infix operators.
   921	fn infix_bp(op: Operator) -> Option<(u8, u8)> {
   922	    match op {
   923	        Operator::Eq
   924	        | Operator::Neq
   925	        | Operator::Lt
   926	        | Operator::Le
   927	        | Operator::Gt
   928	        | Operator::Ge => Some((10, 11)),
   929	        Operator::Concat => Some((20, 21)),
   930	        Operator::Plus | Operator::Minus => Some((30, 31)),
   931	        Operator::Mul | Operator::Div => Some((40, 41)),
   932	        Operator::Pow => Some((51, 50)), // right-assoc: lbp > rbp by 1
   933	        Operator::Percent => None,       // postfix-only
   934	    }
   935	}

   937	/// Canonicalize a function name to uppercase (Excel convention) per CORR-06.
   938	/// Returns an Arc<str> for cheap clone into Expr::Function.name.
   939	fn canonicalize_function_name(name: &Arc<str>) -> Arc<str> {
   940	    let upper = name.as_ref().to_ascii_uppercase();
   941	    Arc::from(upper.as_str())
   942	}

   946	/// Parse the bracket content of a structured reference (e.g. `Sales[...]`)

     1	//! AST printer — produces an A1-canonical string from an `Expr`.
     2	//!
     3	//! Phase B per CORR-20 deliverable: round-trip property `parse(print(parse(src))) ==
     4	//! parse(src)`. The printed form is NOT byte-identical to the source — operator
     5	//! spacing, parenthesization, and function-name casing normalize. The semantic
     6	//! equivalence is what matters (and what the round-trip property captures).
     7	//!
     8	//! ## Parenthesization
     9	//!
    10	//! The printer parenthesizes `Binary` and `Unary` subexpressions whenever the parent's
    11	//! operator binding power would cause re-parsing to misorder the result. The rule:
    12	//!
    13	//! - For a child binary expression with op `c_op`, parenthesize if `bp(c_op).lbp <
    14	//!   parent_min_bp`.
    15	//! - For a power right-operand: parenthesize if `bp(c_op).rbp <= parent_rbp` (since `^`
    16	//!   is right-associative).
    17	//!
    18	//! This produces canonical output: `=1 + 2 * 3` (no parens), `=(1 + 2) * 3` (parens
    19	//! around the addition).
    20	//!
    21	//! ## Round-trip semantics
    22	//!
    23	//! `Number::Number(0.5)` doesn't round-trip to the source `50%` — the lexer normalized
    24	//! percent at lex time. Other normalizations:
    25	//! - Function names → uppercase (`sum(...)` → `SUM(...)`).
    26	//! - Range corners → sorted (`A2:A1` → `A1:A2`).
    27	//! - Whitespace → eliminated except inside string literals.
    28	//!
    29	//! These are intentional canonical-form rewrites, not bugs. The round-trip property
    30	//! checks that `parse(printed) == parse(original)`, NOT that the strings match.
    31	
    32	use crate::ast::{CellAddr, Expr, RangeRef, SheetRef};
    33	use crate::token::Operator;
    34	
    35	/// Print an `Expr` to its A1-canonical string form.
    36	pub fn print(expr: &Expr) -> String {
    37	    let mut out = String::new();
    38	    print_expr(expr, &mut out, 0);
    39	    out
    40	}
    41	
    42	/// Convert a 0-indexed column number to Excel letters (A, B, ..., Z, AA, AB, ...).
    43	fn column_index_to_letters(mut col: u32) -> String {
    44	    let mut letters = String::new();
    45	    // Excel column letters are base-26 with A=1 (not base-26 with A=0). The conversion:
    46	    // 0 → A, 25 → Z, 26 → AA, 27 → AB, ..., 701 → ZZ, 702 → AAA.
    47	    loop {
    48	        let digit = (col % 26) as u8;
    49	        letters.insert(0, (b'A' + digit) as char);
    50	        if col < 26 {
    51	            break;
    52	        }
    53	        col = col / 26 - 1;
    54	    }
    55	    letters
    56	}
    57	
    58	fn print_cell_addr(addr: &CellAddr, out: &mut String) {
    59	    print_sheet_prefix(&addr.sheet, out);
    60	    if addr.abs_col {
    61	        out.push('$');
    62	    }
    63	    out.push_str(&column_index_to_letters(addr.col));
    64	    if addr.abs_row {
    65	        out.push('$');
    66	    }
    67	    // Row source-form is 1-based.
    68	    out.push_str(&(addr.row + 1).to_string());
    69	}

    71	/// **W5-89 (Phase 4.6.A part 3):** emit `Sheet!` or `'Sheet name'!`
    72	/// prefix for non-`Current` sheet refs.
    73	///
    74	/// **Contract:** `print` is pre-bind-only. The expected inputs are:
    75	/// - `SheetRef::Current` — no prefix.
    76	/// - `SheetRef::Name(_)` — the parser's output for a sheet-qualified
    77	///   ref; emits `Sheet!` or `'Sheet name'!`.
    78	///
    79	/// `SheetRef::Id(_)` would require resolving the id back to a name,
    80	/// which means threading workbook context through the printer. That
    81	/// resolver-aware variant is a Phase 4.6.E follow-up polish item
    82	/// tracked in `docs/known-gaps.md` (GAP-X-01). Until then, calling
    83	/// `print` on a post-bind AST is a programmer error and panics with
    84	/// the specific id so the call site is obvious.
    85	///
    86	/// **W5-93 (Phase 4.6.E closure):** Codex MEDIUM and Sonnet MEDIUM
    87	/// converged on this finding. Closure stance: keep the panic (no-
    88	/// fallbacks rule), improve the message, document the contract.
    89	/// The current `rewrite_sheet_name_in_expr` path walks pre-bind
    90	/// ASTs only, so the panic isn't reachable through any production
    91	/// code path today.
    92	fn print_sheet_prefix(sheet: &SheetRef, out: &mut String) {
    93	    match sheet {
    94	        SheetRef::Current => {}
    95	        SheetRef::Name(name) => {
    96	            print_sheet_name(name, out);
    97	            out.push('!');
    98	        }
    99	        SheetRef::Id(id) => panic!(
   100	            "ql_formula_syntax::print: SheetRef::Id({id}) reached the printer, \
   101	             but print() is pre-bind-only by contract (see fn doc comment). \
   102	             The bound id has no associated name string at the AST level; a \
   103	             resolver-aware print_with_resolver(expr, &workbook) API is \
   104	             tracked as Phase 4.6.E follow-up. If you're trying to round-trip \
   105	             a bound expression, re-parse from text instead — the binder \
   106	             does NOT round-trip back through the printer."
   107	        ),
   108	    }
   109	}
   110	
   111	/// **W5-89 (Phase 4.6.A part 3):** emit a sheet name in its canonical
   112	/// source form. Quotes the name if it contains any character outside
   113	/// `[A-Za-z0-9_.]`, starts with a digit (would lex as a number/row),
   114	/// or is empty (defensive — empty names are rejected at registry time
   115	/// anyway). Embedded `'` is escaped as `''`.
   116	fn print_sheet_name(name: &str, out: &mut String) {
   117	    let needs_quoting = name.is_empty()
   118	        || name.starts_with(|c: char| c.is_ascii_digit())
   119	        || name
   120	            .chars()
   121	            .any(|c| !(c.is_ascii_alphanumeric() || c == '_' || c == '.'));
   122	    if needs_quoting {
   123	        out.push('\'');
   124	        for c in name.chars() {
   125	            if c == '\'' {
   126	                out.push('\'');
   127	                out.push('\'');
   128	            } else {
   129	                out.push(c);
   130	            }
   131	        }
   132	        out.push('\'');
   133	    } else {
   134	        out.push_str(name);
   135	    }
   136	}

   138	fn print_range(r: &RangeRef, out: &mut String) {
   139	    match r {
   140	        RangeRef::Cells {
   141	            sheet,
   142	            start_col,
   143	            start_row,
   144	            end_col,
   145	            end_row,
   146	            abs_start_col,
   147	            abs_start_row,
   148	            abs_end_col,
   149	            abs_end_row,
   150	        } => {
   151	            // **W5-89 (Phase 4.6.A part 3):** sheet prefix applies to the
   152	            // WHOLE range, emitted once at the start (Excel canon). The
   153	            // trailing endpoint omits the prefix; both endpoints get
   154	            // `SheetRef::Current` in the synthesized `CellAddr` so
   155	            // `print_cell_addr` emits no prefix internally.
   156	            print_sheet_prefix(sheet, out);
   157	            print_cell_addr(
   158	                &CellAddr {
   159	                    sheet: SheetRef::Current,
   160	                    col: *start_col,
   161	                    row: *start_row,
   162	                    abs_col: *abs_start_col,
   163	                    abs_row: *abs_start_row,
   164	                },
   165	                out,
   166	            );
   167	            out.push(':');
   168	            print_cell_addr(
   169	                &CellAddr {
   170	                    sheet: SheetRef::Current,
   171	                    col: *end_col,
   172	                    row: *end_row,
   173	                    abs_col: *abs_end_col,
   174	                    abs_row: *abs_end_row,
   175	                },
   176	                out,
   177	            );
   178	        }
   179	        RangeRef::WholeColumn {
   180	            sheet,
   181	            start_col,
   182	            end_col,
   183	            abs_start,
   184	            abs_end,
   185	        } => {
   186	            print_sheet_prefix(sheet, out);
   187	            if *abs_start {
   188	                out.push('$');
   189	            }
   190	            out.push_str(&column_index_to_letters(*start_col));
   191	            out.push(':');
   192	            if *abs_end {
   193	                out.push('$');
   194	            }
   195	            out.push_str(&column_index_to_letters(*end_col));
   196	        }
   197	        RangeRef::WholeRow {
   198	            sheet,
   199	            start_row,
   200	            end_row,
   201	            abs_start,
   202	            abs_end,
   203	        } => {
   204	            print_sheet_prefix(sheet, out);
   205	            if *abs_start {
   206	                out.push('$');
   207	            }
   208	            out.push_str(&(*start_row + 1).to_string());
   209	            out.push(':');
   210	            if *abs_end {
   211	                out.push('$');
   212	            }
   213	            out.push_str(&(*end_row + 1).to_string());
   214	        }
   215	    }
   216	}
   217	
   218	fn print_string_literal(s: &str, out: &mut String) {
   219	    out.push('"');
   220	    for ch in s.chars() {
   221	        if ch == '"' {
   222	            out.push('"');
   223	            out.push('"');
   224	        } else {
   225	            out.push(ch);
   226	        }
   227	    }
   228	    out.push('"');
   229	}
   230	
   231	fn print_number(n: f64, out: &mut String) {
   232	    // Use Rust's default f64 Display which matches Excel's general number formatting
   233	    // closely (e.g., 2.0 prints as "2", 2.5 as "2.5", 1e10 as "10000000000").
   234	    if n.fract() == 0.0 && n.abs() < 1.0e16 {
   235	        // Integer-valued f64 → no decimal point.
   236	        out.push_str(&format!("{}", n as i64));
   237	    } else {
   238	        out.push_str(&format!("{n}"));
   239	    }
   240	}

  1002	/// **W5-113 (Phase 4.8.D):** resolve OOXML `'X` escape pairs to literal
  1003	/// `X` in a structured-ref content fragment. Per design § 5.4 the 5
  1004	/// recognized escapes are `'[`, `']`, `'#`, `'@`, `''`; we apply the
  1005	/// rule generically (any `'X` resolves to `X`), matching the lexer's
  1006	/// balancer semantic.
  1007	fn unescape_sref_str(s: &str) -> String {
  1008	    let mut out = String::with_capacity(s.len());
  1009	    let mut chars = s.chars();
  1010	    while let Some(c) = chars.next() {
  1011	        if c == '\'' {
  1012	            // The lexer already guaranteed an escape pair always has
  1013	            // a following char (`DanglingStructuredRefEscape` otherwise).
  1014	            // Defensive: if absent here, drop the `'`.
  1015	            if let Some(escaped) = chars.next() {
  1016	                out.push(escaped);
  1017	            }
  1018	        } else {
  1019	            out.push(c);
  1020	        }
  1021	    }
  1022	    out
  1023	}

  1025	/// Lightweight char cursor over a structured-ref bracket content string.
  1026	struct SrefCursor<'a> {
  1027	    rest: &'a str,
  1028	}
  1029	
  1030	impl<'a> SrefCursor<'a> {
  1031	    fn new(s: &'a str) -> Self {
  1032	        Self { rest: s }
  1033	    }
  1034	    fn peek(&self) -> Option<char> {
  1035	        self.rest.chars().next()
  1036	    }
  1037	    fn advance(&mut self) {
  1038	        if let Some(c) = self.rest.chars().next() {
  1039	            self.rest = &self.rest[c.len_utf8()..];
  1040	        }
  1041	    }
  1042	    fn skip_ws(&mut self) {
  1043	        self.rest = self.rest.trim_start();
  1044	    }
  1045	    fn remaining_trimmed(&self) -> &'a str {
  1046	        self.rest.trim()
  1047	    }
  1048	    fn is_empty(&self) -> bool {
  1049	        self.rest.is_empty()
  1050	    }
  1051	
  1052	    /// Consume a `[...]` group (without leading `[`-consumption — caller
  1053	    /// already consumed the `[`). Returns the inner string PRESERVING
  1054	    /// OOXML escapes (caller resolves them via `unescape_sref_str`).
  1055	    /// The inner `]` is consumed.
  1056	    ///
  1057	    /// **4.8.D refactor:** escape-aware traversal. A `'X` 2-char atom
  1058	    /// is skipped together (so an escaped `]` doesn't close the group).
  1059	    fn consume_bracket_group(&mut self) -> Result<&'a str, &'static str> {
  1060	        let mut depth: u32 = 1;
  1061	        let mut end_byte: Option<usize> = None;
  1062	        let bytes = self.rest.as_bytes();
  1063	        let mut i = 0;
  1064	        while i < bytes.len() {
  1065	            let b = bytes[i];
  1066	            if b == b'\'' {
  1067	                // Skip the 2-char escape atom together. (UTF-8: only ASCII
  1068	                // chars are in the escape set, so byte-offset += 2 works.)
  1069	                i += 2;
  1070	                continue;
  1071	            }
  1072	            match b {
  1073	                b'[' => depth += 1,
  1074	                b']' => {
  1075	                    depth -= 1;
  1076	                    if depth == 0 {
  1077	                        end_byte = Some(i);
  1078	                        break;
  1079	                    }
  1080	                }
  1081	                _ => {}
  1082	            }
  1083	            i += 1;
  1084	        }
  1085	        let end = end_byte.ok_or("unclosed `[` inside structured-ref content")?;
  1086	        let inner = &self.rest[..end];
  1087	        self.rest = &self.rest[end + 1..];
  1088	        Ok(inner.trim())
  1089	    }
  1090	}

  1092	/// Parse the `@...` ThisRow shorthand (caller already consumed the `@`).
  1093	/// Column names are unescaped via `unescape_sref_str`.
  1094	fn parse_sref_thisrow(
  1095	    table_name: &str,
  1096	    bracket_content: &str,
  1097	    cursor: &mut SrefCursor<'_>,
  1098	) -> Result<crate::ast::TableSpecSubtree, ParseError> {
  1099	    cursor.skip_ws();
  1100	    let malformed = |reason: &'static str| ParseError::StructuredRefMalformed {
  1101	        table_name: table_name.to_owned(),
  1102	        bracket_content: bracket_content.to_owned(),
  1103	        reason,
  1104	    };
  1105	
  1106	    // `@Col` (no inner brackets) — single column.
  1107	    if cursor.peek() != Some('[') {
  1108	        let raw = cursor.remaining_trimmed();
  1109	        if raw.is_empty() {
  1110	            return Err(malformed("empty column name after `@`"));
  1111	        }
  1112	        let name = unescape_sref_str(raw);
  1113	        return Ok(crate::ast::TableSpecSubtree::ThisRowColumn(Arc::from(
  1114	            name.as_str(),
  1115	        )));
  1116	    }
  1117	
  1118	    // `@[Col]` or `@[Col1]:[Col2]` — bracketed forms.
  1119	    cursor.advance(); // consume `[`
  1120	    let first_raw = cursor
  1121	        .consume_bracket_group()
  1122	        .map_err(|_| malformed("unclosed `[` after `@`"))?;
  1123	    if first_raw.is_empty() {
  1124	        return Err(malformed("empty column name inside `@[...]`"));
  1125	    }
  1126	    let first = unescape_sref_str(first_raw);
  1127	    cursor.skip_ws();
  1128	    if cursor.is_empty() {
  1129	        return Ok(crate::ast::TableSpecSubtree::ThisRowColumn(Arc::from(
  1130	            first.as_str(),
  1131	        )));
  1132	    }
  1133	    // Expect `:[Col2]`.
  1134	    if cursor.peek() != Some(':') {
  1135	        return Err(malformed("expected `:` or end after `@[Col]`"));
  1136	    }
  1137	    cursor.advance(); // `:`
  1138	    cursor.skip_ws();
  1139	    if cursor.peek() != Some('[') {
  1140	        return Err(malformed("expected `[Col]` after `@[...]:` "));
  1141	    }
  1142	    cursor.advance(); // `[`
  1143	    let second_raw = cursor
  1144	        .consume_bracket_group()
  1145	        .map_err(|_| malformed("unclosed `[` in `@[Col1]:[Col2]`"))?;
  1146	    if second_raw.is_empty() {
  1147	        return Err(malformed("empty second column in `@[Col1]:[Col2]`"));
  1148	    }
  1149	    let second = unescape_sref_str(second_raw);
  1150	    cursor.skip_ws();
  1151	    if !cursor.is_empty() {
  1152	        return Err(malformed("trailing content after `@[Col1]:[Col2]`"));
  1153	    }
  1154	    Ok(crate::ast::TableSpecSubtree::ThisRowColumnRange(
  1155	        Arc::from(first.as_str()),
  1156	        Arc::from(second.as_str()),
  1157	    ))
  1158	}

  1160	/// Parse a `Combination` — one or more `[item]` (or `[Col1]:[Col2]`)
  1161	/// separated by `,`. Caller has NOT yet consumed the first `[`.
  1162	fn parse_sref_combination(
  1163	    table_name: &str,
  1164	    bracket_content: &str,
  1165	    cursor: &mut SrefCursor<'_>,
  1166	) -> Result<crate::ast::TableSpecSubtree, ParseError> {
  1167	    let malformed = |reason: &'static str| ParseError::StructuredRefMalformed {
  1168	        table_name: table_name.to_owned(),
  1169	        bracket_content: bracket_content.to_owned(),
  1170	        reason,
  1171	    };
  1172	
  1173	    let mut items: Vec<crate::ast::TableSpecItem> = Vec::new();
  1174	    loop {
  1175	        cursor.skip_ws();
  1176	        if cursor.peek() != Some('[') {
  1177	            return Err(malformed("expected `[` to open structured-ref item"));
  1178	        }
  1179	        cursor.advance();
  1180	        let inner = cursor
  1181	            .consume_bracket_group()
  1182	            .map_err(|_| malformed("unclosed `[` inside Combination item"))?;
  1183	        let item = classify_sref_item(table_name, bracket_content, inner)?;
  1184	
  1185	        // After this item, peek for `:` (column range) or `,` (next item) or end.
  1186	        cursor.skip_ws();
  1187	        if cursor.peek() == Some(':') {
  1188	            // Column range: only valid if the just-parsed item is a Column.
  1189	            cursor.advance();
  1190	            cursor.skip_ws();
  1191	            if cursor.peek() != Some('[') {
  1192	                return Err(malformed("expected `[Col2]` after `:` in column range"));
  1193	            }
  1194	            cursor.advance();
  1195	            let inner2 = cursor
  1196	                .consume_bracket_group()
  1197	                .map_err(|_| malformed("unclosed `[` in `[Col1]:[Col2]`"))?;
  1198	            let item2 = classify_sref_item(table_name, bracket_content, inner2)?;
  1199	            match (item, item2) {
  1200	                (crate::ast::TableSpecItem::Column(c1), crate::ast::TableSpecItem::Column(c2)) => {
  1201	                    items.push(crate::ast::TableSpecItem::ColumnRange(c1, c2));
  1202	                }
  1203	                _ => {
  1204	                    return Err(malformed(
  1205	                        "`:` between non-column items in structured ref (only `[Col]:[Col]` is valid)",
  1206	                    ));
  1207	                }
  1208	            }
  1209	        } else {
  1210	            items.push(item);
  1211	        }
  1212	
  1213	        cursor.skip_ws();
  1214	        match cursor.peek() {
  1215	            Some(',') => {
  1216	                cursor.advance();
  1217	                continue;
  1218	            }
  1219	            None => break,
  1220	            _ => return Err(malformed("expected `,` or end after structured-ref item")),
  1221	        }
  1222	    }
  1223	
  1224	    Ok(crate::ast::TableSpecSubtree::Combination(items))
  1225	}
  1226	
  1227	/// Classify the inner content of a `[item]` against the 3 item shapes:
  1228	/// `Special(item)`, `Column(name)`, or (caller handles ColumnRange).
  1229	/// Column names are unescaped via `unescape_sref_str`; a leading `'#`
  1230	/// in the raw input is a literal `#` in the column name (NOT a special
  1231	/// item).
  1232	fn classify_sref_item(
  1233	    table_name: &str,
  1234	    bracket_content: &str,
  1235	    inner_raw: &str,
  1236	) -> Result<crate::ast::TableSpecItem, ParseError> {
  1237	    let trimmed_raw = inner_raw.trim();
  1238	    if trimmed_raw.is_empty() {
  1239	        return Err(ParseError::StructuredRefMalformed {
  1240	            table_name: table_name.to_owned(),
  1241	            bracket_content: bracket_content.to_owned(),
  1242	            reason: "empty `[]` item in structured ref",
  1243	        });
  1244	    }
  1245	    // Check whether the first char is an UNESCAPED `#`. A leading `'`
  1246	    // means the `#` (or any other char) is literal.
  1247	    let starts_with_unescaped_hash = trimmed_raw.starts_with('#');
  1248	    if starts_with_unescaped_hash {
  1249	        // Special item: take the substring after `#`, unescape it, and
  1250	        // match the special item set case-insensitively.
  1251	        let after_hash = &trimmed_raw[1..];
  1252	        let rest = unescape_sref_str(after_hash.trim());
  1253	        let special = match rest.to_ascii_uppercase().as_str() {
  1254	            "HEADERS" => crate::ast::SpecialItem::Headers,
  1255	            "TOTALS" => crate::ast::SpecialItem::Totals,
  1256	            "DATA" => crate::ast::SpecialItem::Data,
  1257	            "ALL" => crate::ast::SpecialItem::All,
  1258	            "THIS ROW" => crate::ast::SpecialItem::ThisRow,
  1259	            _ => {
  1260	                return Err(ParseError::StructuredRefMalformed {
  1261	                    table_name: table_name.to_owned(),
  1262	                    bracket_content: bracket_content.to_owned(),
  1263	                    reason: "unknown special item — supported: #Headers, #Totals, #Data, #All, #This Row",
  1264	                });
  1265	            }
  1266	        };
  1267	        Ok(crate::ast::TableSpecItem::Special(special))
  1268	    } else {
  1269	        // Column name. Unescape so a literal `'#Foo` becomes `#Foo`.
  1270	        let name = unescape_sref_str(trimmed_raw);
  1271	        Ok(crate::ast::TableSpecItem::Column(Arc::from(name.as_str())))
  1272	    }
  1273	}

   373	/// **W5-113 (Phase 4.8.D):** apply OOXML structured-reference escapes
   374	/// to a column-name string when emitting it inside `[...]` bracket
   375	/// content. Each of `[`, `]`, `#`, `@`, `'` is preceded by `'`. The
   376	/// lexer's `consume_structured_ref_bracket` reverses this on re-lex.
   377	fn escape_for_sref(name: &str) -> String {
   378	    let mut out = String::with_capacity(name.len());
   379	    for c in name.chars() {
   380	        if matches!(c, '[' | ']' | '#' | '@' | '\'') {
   381	            out.push('\'');
   382	        }
   383	        out.push(c);
   384	    }
   385	    out
   386	}

   388	/// **W5-112 (Phase 4.8.C / 4.8.D):** print the bracket content of a
   389	/// structured reference. Column names are emitted via
   390	/// [`escape_for_sref`] so the round-trip lex(print(parse(x))) = x for
   391	/// any well-formed column name.
   392	fn print_sref_spec(spec: &crate::ast::TableSpecSubtree, out: &mut String) {
   393	    use crate::ast::{SpecialItem, TableSpecItem, TableSpecSubtree};
   394	    match spec {
   395	        TableSpecSubtree::BareColumn(name) => {
   396	            // BareColumn: name is the entire bracket content. Escape
   397	            // any special chars (lexer unescapes).
   398	            out.push_str(&escape_for_sref(name));
   399	        }
   400	        TableSpecSubtree::ThisRowColumn(name) => {
   401	            // Canonical printed form: `@[Col]` (bracketed) so a name
   402	            // starting with `[` or `#` doesn't collide with the
   403	            // unbracketed `@Col` shorthand. Always emit brackets for
   404	            // round-trip stability.
   405	            out.push('@');
   406	            out.push('[');
   407	            out.push_str(&escape_for_sref(name));
   408	            out.push(']');
   409	        }
   410	        TableSpecSubtree::ThisRowColumnRange(c1, c2) => {
   411	            out.push('@');
   412	            out.push('[');
   413	            out.push_str(&escape_for_sref(c1));
   414	            out.push(']');
   415	            out.push(':');
   416	            out.push('[');
   417	            out.push_str(&escape_for_sref(c2));
   418	            out.push(']');
   419	        }
   420	        TableSpecSubtree::Combination(items) => {
   421	            for (i, item) in items.iter().enumerate() {
   422	                if i > 0 {
   423	                    out.push_str(", ");
   424	                }
   425	                match item {
   426	                    TableSpecItem::Special(s) => {
   427	                        out.push('[');
   428	                        out.push_str(match s {
   429	                            SpecialItem::Headers => "#Headers",
   430	                            SpecialItem::Totals => "#Totals",
   431	                            SpecialItem::Data => "#Data",
   432	                            SpecialItem::All => "#All",
   433	                            SpecialItem::ThisRow => "#This Row",
   434	                        });
   435	                        out.push(']');
   436	                    }
   437	                    TableSpecItem::Column(c) => {
   438	                        out.push('[');
   439	                        out.push_str(&escape_for_sref(c));
   440	                        out.push(']');
   441	                    }
   442	                    TableSpecItem::ColumnRange(c1, c2) => {
   443	                        out.push('[');
   444	                        out.push_str(&escape_for_sref(c1));
   445	                        out.push(']');
   446	                        out.push(':');
   447	                        out.push('[');
   448	                        out.push_str(&escape_for_sref(c2));
   449	                        out.push(']');
   450	                    }
   451	                }
   452	            }
   453	        }
   454	    }
   455	}
   456	
   457	/// Binding-power table for binary operators — must match `parser::infix_bp`.
   458	fn binary_bp(op: Operator) -> (u8, u8) {
   459	    match op {
   460	        Operator::Eq
   461	        | Operator::Neq
   462	        | Operator::Lt
   463	        | Operator::Le
   464	        | Operator::Gt
   465	        | Operator::Ge => (10, 11),
   466	        Operator::Concat => (20, 21),
   467	        Operator::Plus | Operator::Minus => (30, 31),
   468	        Operator::Mul | Operator::Div => (40, 41),
   469	        Operator::Pow => (51, 50),
   470	        // Audit L4 fix (2026-05-12): Percent is postfix-only; `binary_bp(Percent)`

  1024	/// Resolve the column span from a list of `Column` / `ColumnRange`
  1025	/// items. Empty list defaults to all columns.
  1026	fn compute_col_range(
  1027	    canonical: &Arc<str>,
  1028	    table: &ql_storage::TableMetadata,
  1029	    col_items: &[&ql_formula_syntax::TableSpecItem],
  1030	) -> Result<(ql_types::ColId, ql_types::ColId), BindError> {
  1031	    use ql_formula_syntax::TableSpecItem;
  1032	    if col_items.is_empty() {
  1033	        // All columns.
  1034	        return Ok((table.top_col, table.top_col + table.cols - 1));
  1035	    }
  1036	    let mut min_col: Option<ql_types::ColId> = None;
  1037	    let mut max_col: Option<ql_types::ColId> = None;
  1038	    for it in col_items {
  1039	        let (lo, hi) = match it {
  1040	            TableSpecItem::Column(col) => {
  1041	                let (idx, _) =
  1042	                    table
  1043	                        .lookup_column(col)
  1044	                        .ok_or_else(|| BindError::UnknownTableColumn {
  1045	                            table: Arc::clone(canonical),
  1046	                            column: Arc::clone(col),
  1047	                        })?;
  1048	                let c = table.top_col + idx;
  1049	                (c, c)
  1050	            }
  1051	            TableSpecItem::ColumnRange(c1, c2) => {
  1052	                let (i1, _) =
  1053	                    table
  1054	                        .lookup_column(c1)
  1055	                        .ok_or_else(|| BindError::UnknownTableColumn {
  1056	                            table: Arc::clone(canonical),
  1057	                            column: Arc::clone(c1),
  1058	                        })?;
  1059	                let (i2, _) =
  1060	                    table
  1061	                        .lookup_column(c2)
  1062	                        .ok_or_else(|| BindError::UnknownTableColumn {
  1063	                            table: Arc::clone(canonical),
  1064	                            column: Arc::clone(c2),
  1065	                        })?;
  1066	                let lo = i1.min(i2);
  1067	                let hi = i1.max(i2);
  1068	                (table.top_col + lo, table.top_col + hi)
  1069	            }
  1070	            TableSpecItem::Special(_) => unreachable!("special items filtered out by caller"),
  1071	        };
  1072	        min_col = Some(min_col.map_or(lo, |m| m.min(lo)));
  1073	        max_col = Some(max_col.map_or(hi, |m| m.max(hi)));
  1074	    }
  1075	    Ok((
  1076	        min_col.expect("at least one column accumulated"),
  1077	        max_col.expect("at least one column accumulated"),
  1078	    ))
  1079	}

  1092	        Expr::Array(_) => "Array",

  1094	        Expr::NameRef(_) => "NameRef",

     1	//! Phase 2B.3 (2026-05-12) — bind-plan cache V0.
     2	//!
     3	//! Caches parsed + bound [`ExprPlan`] values keyed by the formula text, the
     4	//! owning sheet, and the workbook's NameTable generation. Lives on
     5	//! [`crate::WorkbookRuntime`] and is shared into spawned
     6	//! [`crate::WorkbookTransaction`] instances through forwarding.
     7	//!
     8	//! ## Invalidation contract
     9	//!
    10	//! Cache entries are NOT explicitly removed on workbook mutation. Instead,
    11	//! the cache key is `(formula_text, sheet_id, name_table_generation)`. When
    12	//! the workbook mutates state that would change a bind result:
    13	//!
    14	//! - User edits formula text → new text, new key, automatic miss.
    15	//! - Caller registers/removes a name → `NameTable::generation()` advances
    16	//!   (per Phase 2B.3 workbook.rs), so plans bound against the prior
    17	//!   generation no longer match.
    18	//!
    19	//! Stale entries accumulate over time; Phase 3 calcgraph integration adds
    20	//! explicit eviction tied to dirty propagation. For Phase 2B.3 the cache
    21	//! is small enough that drift is acceptable — typical workbook formula
    22	//! cardinality is in the thousands, not millions.
    23	//!
    24	//! ## What the cache stores
    25	//!
    26	//! `Arc<ExprPlan>` so cache hits hand back a refcount-bump without cloning
    27	//! the plan tree. `ExprPlan` is `Clone + Debug + PartialEq` per
    28	//! `ql-exec::plan` but the plan tree is non-trivial; sharing via `Arc`
    29	//! keeps the cache cheap.
    30	//!
    31	//! ## References
    32	//!
    33	//! - `.references/hyperformula/src/parser/ParserWithCaching.ts` — HF caches
    34	//!   parsed ASTs keyed by formula text + sheet metadata for the same
    35	//!   reason. They evict on text change; we additionally include
    36	//!   `NameTable::generation` so the bind result (not just the parse) stays
    37	//!   consistent.
    38	//! - `.references/hyperformula/src/parser/Cache.ts` — the size-bounded LRU
    39	//!   wrapper. V0 here is unbounded; a bounded eviction policy lands when
    40	//!   workbook size makes it necessary.
    41	
    42	use std::collections::HashMap;
    43	use std::sync::Arc;
    44	
    45	use ql_types::SheetId;
    46	
    47	use crate::plan::ExprPlan;
    48	
    49	/// Composite cache key. Reflects everything that, if changed, would change
    50	/// the bind result for a given formula text:
    51	///
    52	/// - `text` — the raw formula source (without leading `=`). Different text
    53	///   yields a different parse tree, so plans don't share.
    54	/// - `sheet` — owning sheet for the binding. Same text in different sheets
    55	///   binds to different cell references (relative refs resolve against the
    56	///   owning sheet).
    57	/// - `name_gen` — `NameTable::generation()` at the moment of bind. A
    58	///   subsequent name registration / removal bumps this, so old plans
    59	///   become unreachable.
    60	#[derive(Clone, Debug, Hash, Eq, PartialEq)]
    61	pub struct PlanCacheKey {
    62	    pub text: Arc<str>,
    63	    pub sheet: SheetId,
    64	    pub name_gen: u64,
    65	}
    66	
    67	/// Snapshot of cache observability data — exposed via
    68	/// `WorkbookRuntime::cache_stats()` and surfaced through `ql-profile`.
    69	#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, Default, PartialEq, Eq)]
    70	pub struct PlanCacheStats {
    71	    pub hits: u64,
    72	    pub misses: u64,
    73	    pub entries: usize,
    74	}
    75	
    76	impl PlanCacheStats {
    77	    /// Hit rate as a fraction in `[0.0, 1.0]`. Returns `None` when no
    78	    /// lookups have happened yet so callers don't divide by zero or
    79	    /// display "0.0%" for an unused cache.
    80	    pub fn hit_rate(&self) -> Option<f64> {
    81	        let total = self.hits + self.misses;
    82	        if total == 0 {
    83	            None
    84	        } else {
    85	            Some(self.hits as f64 / total as f64)
    86	        }
    87	    }
    88	}
    89	
    90	/// Phase 2B.3 bind-plan cache.
    91	///
    92	/// Stores `Arc<ExprPlan>` per `(text, sheet, name_gen)` key. Hits return
    93	/// the cached plan (refcount-bump only); misses run the caller-supplied
    94	/// build closure. Accumulates hit/miss counters for observability.
    95	///
    96	/// `Default` constructs an empty cache. The cache is opt-in via
    97	/// `WorkbookRuntime::with_plan_cache_disabled` — not yet implemented; the
    98	/// default runtime constructor builds an empty cache that gets populated
    99	/// on first lookup.
   100	#[derive(Debug, Default)]
   101	pub struct PlanCache {
   102	    entries: HashMap<PlanCacheKey, Arc<ExprPlan>>,
   103	    hits: u64,
   104	    misses: u64,
   105	}
   106	
   107	impl PlanCache {
   108	    /// Empty cache. Same as `Default::default`.
   109	    pub fn new() -> Self {
   110	        Self::default()
   111	    }
   112	
   113	    /// Look up the plan for `key`. On hit, returns the cached `Arc<ExprPlan>`
   114	    /// and increments the hit counter. On miss, runs `build()` (which does
   115	    /// the lex/parse/bind), stores the result, and returns it. Errors from
   116	    /// `build()` are passed through without caching — failed binds aren't
   117	    /// memoized, so a fix to the formula text gets a clean retry.
   118	    pub fn get_or_insert<F, E>(&mut self, key: PlanCacheKey, build: F) -> Result<Arc<ExprPlan>, E>
   119	    where
   120	        F: FnOnce() -> Result<ExprPlan, E>,
   121	    {
   122	        if let Some(plan) = self.entries.get(&key) {
   123	            self.hits += 1;
   124	            return Ok(Arc::clone(plan));
   125	        }
   126	        self.misses += 1;
   127	        let plan = Arc::new(build()?);
   128	        self.entries.insert(key, Arc::clone(&plan));
   129	        Ok(plan)
   130	    }

     1	//! Cell-read environment for the scalar evaluator.
     2	//!
     3	//! The evaluator needs to look up cell values when it encounters `ExprPlan::CellRef`.
     4	//! Rather than coupling to `ql-storage::Workbook` directly, we abstract via a trait so:
     5	//!
     6	//! - Test harnesses can supply a `HashMap`-backed fake environment.
     7	//! - The Week 4+ runtime can wrap a real `Workbook` + `ColumnStore` chain.
     8	//! - Future cross-workbook references (Phase 6+) can implement the trait against an
     9	//!   external dataset adapter.
    10	//!
    11	//! The trait is intentionally narrow: just `read_cell`. Sheet-/column-bulk reads belong on
    12	//! `ql-storage` directly and the SIMD path (W4-2) doesn't go through this trait at all —
    13	//! it reads Arrow chunks via `ColumnStore::iter_chunks` for batch processing.
    14	
    15	use ql_storage::{NameTable, NamedTarget};
    16	use ql_types::{
    17	    ColId, ErrorValue, EvalContext, Range, RowId, SheetId, Value, DEFAULT_EVAL_CONTEXT,
    18	};
    19	
    20	use crate::plan::{NameLookup, ResolvedName};
