    22	/// Read a single cell value. Out-of-bounds reads return `Value::Blank` per Excel semantics.
    23	pub trait CellEnv {
    24	    fn read_cell(&self, sheet: SheetId, row: RowId, col: ColId) -> Value;
    25	
    26	    /// Phase 3.6 (2026-05-12) — AGG-3-04 entry point. Materialize a range
    27	    /// into a flat `Vec<Value>` for aggregate functions (SUM, AVERAGE,
    28	    /// MIN, MAX, COUNT, PRODUCT). Default impl iterates the range row by
    29	    /// row using `read_cell`; impls that have cheaper bounds info (e.g.
    30	    /// `WorkbookEnv` clamps to `Sheet::bounds`) override.
    31	    ///
    32	    /// Returns an empty Vec for a degenerate range (e.g. range entirely
    33	    /// outside a sheet's populated area). The aggregate function then
    34	    /// applies its empty-input rule (MIN/MAX → 0; AVERAGE → `#DIV/0!`;
    35	    /// SUM/COUNT/PRODUCT → 0 or 1; see `ql-functions::scalar_fns`).
    36	    fn read_range(&self, range: Range) -> Vec<Value> {
    37	        let mut out = Vec::new();
    38	        for row in range.start_row..=range.end_row {
    39	            for col in range.start_col..=range.end_col {
    40	                out.push(self.read_cell(range.sheet, row, col));
    41	            }
    42	            // Safety: end_row could be `RowId::MAX` (whole-column); the
    43	            // outer range syntax must be bounded by the caller. The
    44	            // default impl iterates to completion — the WorkbookEnv
    45	            // override clamps to `Sheet::bounds().row_extent`.
    46	        }
    47	        out
    48	    }
    49	
    50	    /// **W5-54 (range-aware functions, GAP-F-05 follow-up):** return
    51	    /// the flat `Vec<Value>` for the range PLUS its 2D shape (rows,
    52	    /// cols). For range-aware functions like VLOOKUP/INDEX which
    53	    /// need to address by `(row, col)`, the dispatcher uses this to
    54	    /// construct `FnArg::Range { values, rows, cols }`.
    55	    ///
    56	    /// `rows * cols == values.len()` is the invariant. If the env
    57	    /// clamps to sheet bounds (`WorkbookEnv`), the returned shape
    58	    /// reflects the clamped dimensions. The default impl below
    59	    /// returns the unclamped shape derived from the range bounds —
    60	    /// matching the unclamped `read_range` default.
    61	    fn read_range_with_shape(&self, range: Range) -> (Vec<Value>, usize, usize) {
    62	        let values = self.read_range(range);
    63	        let rows = (range.end_row - range.start_row + 1) as usize;
    64	        let cols = (range.end_col - range.start_col + 1) as usize;
    65	        debug_assert_eq!(rows.saturating_mul(cols), values.len());
    66	        (values, rows, cols)
    67	    }
    68	
    69	    /// **W5-69 (Phase 4.5.A.0):** the evaluator context for date /
    70	    /// locale / clock-aware function dispatch. Default impl returns

    72	    /// for tests, MapEnv, and benches that don't carry workbook state.
    73	    /// `WorkbookEnv` overrides this with the workbook's actual
    74	    /// `date_system` field once Sub-phase 4.5.A adds it.
    75	    fn eval_context(&self) -> &EvalContext {
    76	        &DEFAULT_EVAL_CONTEXT
    77	    }
    78

    85	    }

    88	/// `ql-storage::Workbook`-backed implementation. Wraps a Workbook reference; reads dispatch
    89	/// via the Workbook's sheet lookup + the sheet's `read(row, col)`.
    90	///
    91	/// **W5-71 (Phase 4.5.A.2):** caches an `EvalContext` built from the

    94	/// Phase-6.3 WASM bindings will plumb those through too.
    95	pub struct WorkbookEnv<'w> {
    96	    workbook: &'w ql_storage::Workbook,
    97	    eval_ctx: EvalContext,
    98	    /// **W5-117 (Phase 4.8.G.2):** the formula's own cell address, if

   102	    /// range). `None` for paths without cell context (legacy callers,
   103	    /// tests).
   104	    formula_cell: Option<ql_types::Address>,
   105	}
   106	
   107	impl<'w> WorkbookEnv<'w> {
   108	    pub fn new(workbook: &'w ql_storage::Workbook) -> Self {
   109	        let eval_ctx = EvalContext {
   110	            date_system: workbook.date_system(),
   111	            ..EvalContext::default()
   112	        };
   113	        Self {
   114	            workbook,
   115	            eval_ctx,

   117	        }
   118	    }
   119	
   120	    /// **W5-117 (Phase 4.8.G.2):** WorkbookEnv variant that carries the
   121	    /// formula's own cell. Used at eval time when the caller knows
   122	    /// the cell (set_formula, recompute_dirty, recompute_all,
   123	    /// validate_formula).
   124	    pub fn with_formula_cell(workbook: &'w ql_storage::Workbook, cell: ql_types::Address) -> Self {
   125	        let mut env = Self::new(workbook);

   127	        env
   128	    }
   129	
   130	    /// **W5-117 (Phase 4.8.G.2):** the formula's cell address, if the
   131	    /// env was constructed with one. Used by the structured-ref `[@Col]`
   132	    /// narrowing path in scalar.rs.
   133	    pub fn formula_cell(&self) -> Option<ql_types::Address> {
   134	        self.formula_cell
   135	    }
   136	}

   139	    fn read_cell(&self, sheet: SheetId, row: RowId, col: ColId) -> Value {
   140	        // Phase 2A.7 audit H6 (2026-05-12): out-of-bounds sheet now surfaces
   141	        // `Value::Error(ErrorValue::Ref)` — Excel's canonical `#REF!`. Prior
   142	        // behavior silently mapped missing-sheet to `Value::Blank`, which
   143	        // hid stale-sheet refs in saved formulas (e.g., a formula authored
   144	        // against Sheet3 in a workbook later truncated to 2 sheets). Two
   145	        // megaudit agents flagged the silent fallback. Within an existing
   146	        // sheet, missing cells still return `Blank` — that's correct Excel
   147	        // canon for "empty cell."
   148	        match self.workbook.sheet(sheet) {
   149	            Some(s) => s.read(row, col),
   150	            None => Value::Error(ErrorValue::Ref),
   151	        }
   152	    }
   153	
   154	    /// Phase 3.6 override: clamps the iteration to `Sheet::bounds` so a
   155	    /// `SUM(A:A)` named range with `end_row = RowId::MAX` reads only the
   156	    /// populated rows (typically a few hundred thousand at most on a
   157	    /// real sheet), not all 4 billion `RowId` slots. Without this clamp

   159	    /// usable) would hang at evaluation time.
   160	    fn read_range(&self, range: Range) -> Vec<Value> {
   161	        let Some(sheet) = self.workbook.sheet(range.sheet) else {
   162	            return vec![Value::Error(ErrorValue::Ref)];
   163	        };
   164	        let bounds = sheet.bounds();
   165	        // bounds extents are "one past max"; convert to inclusive bounds.
   166	        if bounds.row_extent == 0 || bounds.col_extent == 0 {
   167	            return Vec::new();
   168	        }
   169	        let max_row = bounds.row_extent - 1;
   170	        let max_col = bounds.col_extent - 1;
   171	        let end_row = range.end_row.min(max_row);
   172	        let end_col = range.end_col.min(max_col);
   173	        if range.start_row > end_row || range.start_col > end_col {
   174	            return Vec::new();
   175	        }
   176	        let mut out = Vec::with_capacity(
   177	            ((end_row - range.start_row + 1) as usize)
   178	                .saturating_mul((end_col - range.start_col + 1) as usize),
   179	        );
   180	        for row in range.start_row..=end_row {
   181	            for col in range.start_col..=end_col {
   182	                out.push(sheet.read(row, col));
   183	            }
   184	        }
   185	        out
   186	    }

   399	                                    Ok(r) => flat.extend(env.read_range(r)),
   400	                                    Err(ev) => flat.push(Value::Error(ev)),
   401	                                },
   402	                                ExprPlan::Array(rows) => {
   403	                                    // **W5-100:** array cells are literal-
   404	                                    // only per the W5-99 binder restriction
   405	                                    // (Number / Bool / String / Error). Eval
   406	                                    // each cell via the scalar path — the
   407	                                    // legacy scalar arm already returns the
   408	                                    // literal value via `eval_scalar_with_cache`.
   409	                                    // Row-major iteration matches the
   410	                                    // existing `env.read_range` flat shape.

   164	    pub fn row_count(&self) -> u64 {
   165	        self.base_chunks.iter().map(|c| c.len() as u64).sum()
   166	    }
   167	
   168	    /// Read a single cell. Out-of-bounds returns `Value::Blank`.
   169	    /// Cascade (Phase 3.5): `user_overlay` → `computed_overlay` → `base`.
   170	    pub fn read(&self, row: RowId) -> Value {
   171	        let (chunk_idx, rel_row) = self.locate(row);
   172	        if chunk_idx >= self.base_chunks.len() {
   173	            return Value::Blank;
   174	        }
   175	        // 1. User overlay.
   176	        if let Some(v) = self.user_overlays[chunk_idx].get(rel_row) {
   177	            return v.clone();
   178	        }
   179	        // 2. Computed overlay (formula outputs).
   180	        if let Some(v) = self.computed_overlays[chunk_idx].get(rel_row) {
   181	            return v.clone();
   182	        }
   183	        // 3. Base.
   184	        let base = &self.base_chunks[chunk_idx];
   185	        if (rel_row as usize) >= base.len() {
   186	            // Within an under-sized last chunk's tail: read as Blank.
   187	            return Value::Blank;
   188	        }
   189	        read_base(base.as_ref(), rel_row)
   190	    }

   192	    /// Write a USER-edit cell. Chunk autogrowth: if `row` is beyond all current chunks,

   195	    /// model: writing to A1000 of an empty column creates the chunks containing it.
   196	    ///
   197	    /// Phase 3.5: ALSO clears any prior `computed_overlay` entry at `row` — a fresh user

codex
The first concrete issue is emerging around table footprint bounds: runtime and replay validate non-zero dimensions, but not that `top + size - 1` stays inside the Excel grid, while the loader repeats the same gap for persisted metadata. I’m checking whether there are more normal-use consequences before assigning severity.

   578	    let reg = default_registry();

   583	            .unwrap();
   584	    }

   592	    }

  3588	    // ===== W5-123 (Phase 4.8.L) — schema v6 + TableTable persistence =====
  3589	
  3590	    fn wb_with_table(table_name: &str, columns: &[&str]) -> Workbook {
  3591	        use ql_storage::{TableColumn, TableMetadata};
  3592	        let mut wb = Workbook::new();
  3593	        let sheet_id = wb.add_sheet("Data");
  3594	        let canonical_lower: Vec<Arc<str>> = columns
  3595	            .iter()
  3596	            .map(|c| Arc::from(c.to_ascii_lowercase().as_str()))
  3597	            .collect();
  3598	        let display: Vec<Arc<str>> = columns.iter().map(|c| Arc::from(*c)).collect();
  3599	        let cols: Vec<TableColumn> = canonical_lower
  3600	            .iter()
  3601	            .zip(display.iter())
  3602	            .map(|(n, d)| TableColumn {
  3603	                id: wb.tables_mut().allocate_column_id(),
  3604	                name: Arc::clone(n),
  3605	                display: Arc::clone(d),
  3606	                totals_function: None,
  3607	            })
  3608	            .collect();
  3609	        let canonical_upper: Arc<str> = Arc::from(table_name.to_ascii_uppercase().as_str());
  3610	        let display_name: Arc<str> = Arc::from(table_name);
  3611	        let meta = TableMetadata {
  3612	            name: Arc::clone(&canonical_upper),
  3613	            display_name,
  3614	            sheet: sheet_id,
  3615	            top_row: 0,
  3616	            top_col: 0,
  3617	            rows: 3,
  3618	            cols: columns.len() as u32,
  3619	            has_header: true,
  3620	            has_totals: false,
  3621	            columns: cols,
  3622	        };
  3623	        wb.tables_mut().insert(canonical_upper, meta);
  3624	        wb
  3625	    }

  3627	    #[test]
  3628	    fn roundtrip_with_single_table_preserves_metadata() {
  3629	        let dir = TempDir::new().unwrap();
  3630	        let path = dir.path().join("test.qbook");
  3631	        let wb = wb_with_table("Sales", &["Qty", "Price"]);
  3632	        let pre_meta = wb.lookup_table("Sales").unwrap().clone();
  3633	        save_workbook(&wb, "test", &path).unwrap();
  3634	
  3635	        let loaded = load_workbook(&path).unwrap();
  3636	        let post = loaded.lookup_table("Sales").expect("table reloaded");
  3637	        assert_eq!(post.name, pre_meta.name);
  3638	        assert_eq!(post.display_name, pre_meta.display_name);
  3639	        assert_eq!(post.sheet, pre_meta.sheet);
  3640	        assert_eq!(post.top_row, pre_meta.top_row);
  3641	        assert_eq!(post.top_col, pre_meta.top_col);
  3642	        assert_eq!(post.rows, pre_meta.rows);
  3643	        assert_eq!(post.cols, pre_meta.cols);
  3644	        assert_eq!(post.has_header, pre_meta.has_header);
  3645	        assert_eq!(post.has_totals, pre_meta.has_totals);
  3646	        assert_eq!(post.columns.len(), pre_meta.columns.len());
  3647	        for (a, b) in post.columns.iter().zip(pre_meta.columns.iter()) {
  3648	            assert_eq!(a.id, b.id, "column ids must survive verbatim");
  3649	            assert_eq!(a.name, b.name);
  3650	            assert_eq!(a.display, b.display);
  3651	            assert_eq!(a.totals_function, b.totals_function);
  3652	        }
  3653	    }

  3655	    #[test]
  3656	    fn roundtrip_with_multiple_tables_preserves_each() {
  3657	        use ql_storage::{TableColumn, TableMetadata};
  3658	        let dir = TempDir::new().unwrap();
  3659	        let path = dir.path().join("test.qbook");
  3660	        let mut wb = Workbook::new();
  3661	        let sheet_id = wb.add_sheet("Sheet1");
  3662	        // Two tables on the same sheet at non-overlapping locations.
  3663	        for (name, top_row) in &[("Sales", 0u32), ("Orders", 10u32)] {
  3664	            let id = wb.tables_mut().allocate_column_id();
  3665	            let canonical: Arc<str> = Arc::from(name.to_ascii_uppercase().as_str());
  3666	            wb.tables_mut().insert(
  3667	                Arc::clone(&canonical),
  3668	                TableMetadata {
  3669	                    name: Arc::clone(&canonical),
  3670	                    display_name: Arc::from(*name),
  3671	                    sheet: sheet_id,
  3672	                    top_row: *top_row,
  3673	                    top_col: 0,
  3674	                    rows: 3,
  3675	                    cols: 1,
  3676	                    has_header: true,
  3677	                    has_totals: false,
  3678	                    columns: vec![TableColumn {
  3679	                        id,
  3680	                        name: Arc::from("qty"),
  3681	                        display: Arc::from("Qty"),
  3682	                        totals_function: None,
  3683	                    }],
  3684	                },
  3685	            );
  3686	        }
  3687	        save_workbook(&wb, "test", &path).unwrap();
  3688	        let loaded = load_workbook(&path).unwrap();
  3689	        assert!(loaded.lookup_table("Sales").is_some());
  3690	        assert!(loaded.lookup_table("Orders").is_some());
  3691	        assert_eq!(loaded.lookup_table("Sales").unwrap().top_row, 0);
  3692	        assert_eq!(loaded.lookup_table("Orders").unwrap().top_row, 10);
  3693	    }

  3695	    #[test]
  3696	    fn roundtrip_with_totals_function_preserves_each_variant() {
  3697	        use ql_storage::{TableColumn, TableMetadata, TotalsFunction};

  3703	            TotalsFunction::None,
  3704	            TotalsFunction::Average,
  3705	            TotalsFunction::Count,
  3706	            TotalsFunction::CountNums,
  3707	            TotalsFunction::Max,
  3708	            TotalsFunction::Min,
  3709	            TotalsFunction::StdDev,
  3710	            TotalsFunction::Sum,
  3711	            TotalsFunction::Variance,
  3712	            TotalsFunction::Custom,
  3713	        ];
  3714	        let columns: Vec<TableColumn> = variants
  3715	            .iter()
  3716	            .enumerate()
  3717	            .map(|(i, t)| TableColumn {
  3718	                id: wb.tables_mut().allocate_column_id(),
  3719	                name: Arc::from(format!("c{i}").as_str()),
  3720	                display: Arc::from(format!("C{i}").as_str()),
  3721	                totals_function: Some(*t),
  3722	            })
  3723	            .collect();
  3724	        let canonical: Arc<str> = Arc::from("ALLTOTALS");
  3725	        wb.tables_mut().insert(
  3726	            Arc::clone(&canonical),
  3727	            TableMetadata {
  3728	                name: Arc::clone(&canonical),
  3729	                display_name: Arc::from("AllTotals"),
  3730	                sheet: sheet_id,
  3731	                top_row: 0,
  3732	                top_col: 0,
  3733	                rows: 3,
  3734	                cols: variants.len() as u32,
  3735	                has_header: true,
  3736	                has_totals: true,
  3737	                columns,
  3738	            },
  3739	        );
  3740	        save_workbook(&wb, "test", &path).unwrap();
  3741	        let loaded = load_workbook(&path).unwrap();

  3743	        for (i, v) in variants.iter().enumerate() {

  3746	                Some(*v),

   768	fn save_load_workbook_with_table_and_formula_roundtrips() {

   777	        rt.set_value(0, 2, 0, Value::Number(20.0)).unwrap();

   795	        .expect("table metadata reloaded");
   796	    assert_eq!(meta.rows, 3);
   797	    assert_eq!(meta.cols, 1);
   798	    assert!(meta.has_header);

codex
**Findings**

- **HIGH** `crates/ql-exec/src/workbook_runtime.rs:1516-1587`, `crates/ql-exec/src/workbook_runtime.rs:1963-2054`, `crates/ql-oplog/src/replay.rs:890-917`, `crates/ql-oplog/src/replay.rs:694-795`, `crates/ql-io/src/qbook_format.rs:1700-1750`  
  Table create/resize/replay/load validate non-zero dimensions but not that `top_row + rows - 1` and `top_col + cols - 1` stay within `MAX_ROW`/`MAX_COLUMN`; normal runtime calls can register out-of-grid tables or trigger huge/overflowing footprint walks.  
  Fix: add one checked footprint validator using `checked_add(rows - 1)` / `checked_add(cols - 1)` and enforce it in producer create/resize, replay create/resize, and v6 table load before any footprint loops or inserts.

- **MEDIUM** `crates/ql-io/src/qbook_format.rs:1700-1750`, `crates/ql-storage/src/tables.rs:303-308`  
  The v6 loader accepts table metadata that runtime creation would reject, including duplicate canonical table names silently overwriting prior entries, table/name namespace collisions, overlapping table footprints, empty/duplicate column names, and mismatched column canonical/display forms.  
  Fix: before `tables_mut().insert`, validate the same structural registry invariants as runtime creation and return `QbookError::MalformedTable` on the first violation.

- **MEDIUM** `crates/ql-storage/src/tables.rs:303-306`  
  `TableTable::insert` computes `max_id + 1` unchecked; a persisted column id of `u32::MAX` panics in debug or wraps in release, after which the allocator can collide.  
  Fix: reject `u32::MAX` column ids during qbook load with `MalformedTable`, or make `insert` return a visible error instead of doing unchecked allocator advancement.

- **LOW** `crates/ql-exec/src/workbook_runtime.rs:1963-2093`  
  `resize_table` emits `Op::ResizeTable`, bumps table generation, and clears the plan cache even for exact no-op calls where dimensions and column changes are unchanged.  
  Fix: after validation/snapshot, return `Ok(())` without op emission when `new_rows == old_rows && new_cols == old_cols && added_columns.is_empty() && removed_columns.is_empty()`.

- **LOW** `crates/ql-exec/tests/oplog_e2e.rs:649-759`, `crates/ql-exec/tests/oplog_e2e.rs:768-811`  
  Coverage has separate rename-column replay, resize replay, and table save/load tests, but no composed `create_table -> rename_column -> resize_table` replay test and no save/load followed by `rename_column` formula-rewrite test.  
  Fix: add one composed op-log replay test where a renamed trailing column is removed by resize, plus one persistence e2e that reloads then renames a table column and verifies formula text/value parity.

**Notes**

String-literal rewrite and escaped `'#Headers` handling look correct: rewrite walks the parsed AST, leaves `Expr::String` untouched, and `TableSpecItem::Special` is distinct from escaped `TableSpecItem::Column`. Generation bumps are present on the normal mutation paths, and existing tests cover allocator preservation past loaded ids.

I attempted targeted `cargo test` runs, but this environment has no `cargo` on `PATH`, so I could not re-run tests locally.

**NOT SHIP-READY**
